# Definitions of Human Actions and Divine Concepts

**Involuntary action:** That which has its beginning from without, or that which has its beginning from within, where the rational soul <note>original: "andzn ishkhanakan" - literally "the governing self" or "sovereign soul"</note> does not act by its own will. **Involuntary** is that which has its beginning from without, where no assistance was given according to its own natural inclination. **Involuntary** is that action which is followed by mercy and forgiveness, and by sorrow, and never by pleasure. **Involuntary** is that which is not willful, but is accompanied by grief and regret. **Involuntary through ignorance** is that which is completed through seeking; for instance, when we unknowingly kill an enemy or find a treasure, we say these are not by will, nor are they from a terrible fear. <margin>L</margin> **Clamor** is the act of taking by will and with a loud voice. **Prayer** is the asking for good things from God. **Prayer** is the turning of the mind toward the Holy One with humble devotion. <margin>Ch</margin> **Prayer** is the lifting of the soul from earthly things to heavenly ones. **Prayer** is the offering of appropriate petitions to God. **The Soul's motion** is self-moved according to its greatness. **Pride** is a movement of the mind by which one is moved to boast of having that which he does not possess, or claiming to have it from himself. **Pride** was not a cause of good, but a wickedness of falsehood. **Shame** is the fear of

<column-break/>

approaching reproach; or **shame** is a prolonged modesty; and **modesty** is a milder form of shame. **Shame** is the fear of ill-repute. **Shame** is found alongside modesty; and **modesty** is the turning back without resistance from **shame**. **Marriage** is the union of man and woman for the procreation of children and the fellowship of this life. **Conjunction** <note>original: "Anmiak" - likely referring to astronomical conjunction</note> is from the traveling together of the moon and the sun, one following the other. <margin>A</margin> **Alteration** is a movement from a place in intensity. **A man** is the primary source of seed. **A vineyard** is the parent-plant of wine. <margin>A</margin> **A name** is the voice of the doer or the receiver; or a significant voice according to a paired composition without time, of which no part signifies anything when separated. **A name** is a significant voice interpreted according to the will, of which no part signifies anything specifically or directly. **A name** is that which signifies a substance along with a quality. **Incomplete** is that which is composed of parts whose perfection is not seen in others. **Without beginning** by nature is the First One, who is the Creator of beginnings. **Unknown** is that which is hidden.

<summary>This page provides a series of philosophical definitions regarding the "Involuntary" (actions taken without consent or through ignorance), the nature of "Prayer," and the definitions of "Pride," "Shame," and "Marriage." It also includes linguistic definitions of what constitutes a "Name" (Noun).</summary>
<keywords>Involuntary, Prayer, Pride, Shame, Marriage, Definition of Names, Armenian Philosophy, Ethics, Theology</keywords>

# Definitions of Moral Failings and the Nature of the Person

...of faith. And **Love of Opinion** <note>original: "Kartsu't'asirut'iwn" - a tendency to value one's own prejudices or assumptions over truth</note> is to construct what they did not have, just as anyone might wish to have it. 

**Impudence** [or Folly] is the slandering of these matters; a willful act through practical works, or an ignorance of things written. **Impudence** cannot venture into those areas. 

**Unrighteousness** [Injustice] is the external operation of the parts of the soul; a willful act of deprivation, or an ignorance of the consequences. 

**Inequality** is the fate that brings to light the power of each individual. 

**The Imperishable** is an eternal shadow, appearing like a shade; or it is the good of someone... <note>The text here uses a metaphor of light and night to describe the nature of eternal things versus temporary perceptions.</note> now becoming night. 

**The Unordered** are those things which are always carried in the same way from the East to the West. 

**Disobedience** is a stubbornness with a crippled mind, refusing to obey those whom one ought to obey. 

**Shamelessness** is the denial of appropriate honor to the one to whom it is owed. 

**Curse** is the seeking of evil. 

**Injustice** is the foreknowledge of lawlessness in some and the preparation for condemnations. 

**Retraction** <note>original: "Vermerandu't'iwn" - here meaning a cooling of fervor or a turning back</note> is the turning back from the pursuit of the desires one has already taken up. 

**Imprudent zeal** is that which is not governed by reason. 

**Impatience** is the sullen attack of the soul for the sake of avenging injustices.

<column-break/>

**Defective zeal** is leaving the work half-finished due to numbness. 

**Resentment** <note>original: "Nokhakalut'iwn" - specifically the harboring of a grudge</note> is that by which the movement of the soul toward hatred for someone—arising from self-stirred disturbances—is restrained through a kind of wakefulness and sobriety. 

**Resentment** is a stillness of the heart that possesses a power mixed with vengeance. 

**Resentment** [in a positive sense] is the sweetness of a greater person toward a lesser one who deserves punishment. 

**Self-determination** [Free Will] <note>original: "Andzn-ishkhanut'iwn" - literally "sovereignty over oneself." This is a foundational concept in Armenian theology regarding the human capacity to choose between good and evil.</note> is the strong spirit of the reason and the will, by which it seeks the good, staying close to grace as it was in the beginning. 

**Self-determination** is the authority of the reason and the will, by which the good is chosen by being near to grace, and the evil is avoided by being far from grace. 

**A Person** [The Self] <note>original: "Andzn" - in this philosophical context, it corresponds to the Greek "Hypostasis," referring to an individual existence or a distinct being.</note> is a nature of intellectual, uncommunicable subsistence. 

**A Person** is an uncommunicable subsistence of a rational nature. 

**A Person** is a subject <note>original: "ent'adrut'iwn" - a literal translation of the Greek "hypostasis" (that which is placed under)</note> distinguished by a unique property, possessing dignity. 

**A Person** is the primary substance of an intellectual nature. 

**A Person** is an indivisible substance of a rational nature. 

**A Person** is a subject according to itself alone; a representation of a unique nature and substance. 

**A Person** is a subject distinguished by a unique property.

**Vocabulary:** Impudence, Unrighteousness, Inequality, Disobedience, Person [Hypostasis], Authority [Sovereignty], Nature, Substance, Subject [Hypostasis].

<meta>This page continues the sequence of philosophical and theological definitions, moving from the analysis of human character into broader cosmological, logical, and ethical categories. It reflects the influence of the "Grecophile" school of Armenian scholars (5th–8th centuries), who translated and adapted works by Aristotle, Porphyry, and David the Invincible.</meta>

...relating to the honorable rational [soul].

<margin>7</margin> **World** <note>original: "Ashkharh"</note> is the establishment that is in heaven and on earth; and of the natures that are within them, or of the great sentient creatures, encompassing all life or possessing the totality of all things individually. It also concerns the breath of time <margin>8</margin> in which power grows strong. 

**Corruption** <note>original: "Apakanut'iwn" - refers to physical decay, destruction, or the philosophical opposite of "becoming" (generation)</note> is the opposite of becoming. **Corruption** is a movement toward non-existence. Corruptible thoughts [remain] after death, under which fall the lifeless, the non-sentient, and the non-personal. 

**Proof** <note>original: "Apats'oyts'" - a logical demonstration</note> is a composition of words from the soul, gathered from the first, the middle, and the true; or words through which known things are shown from those which are not known through them. 

**Rebellion** <note>original: "Apstambut'iwn" - here defined first in a positive sense of "standing firm" or "fortitude"</note> is a virtue by which the soul is established within itself toward great and modest things through hope. 

**Rebellion** is to oppose by deviating from the authority of the greater ones. 

**Repentance** <note>original: "Apashkharut'iwn" - penance or changing one's mind</note> is to lament past evil and to no longer do what those [evils] are. 

**Repentance** is to weep for sins committed previously and likewise to do good. 

**Virtue** <note>original: "Arak'inut'iwn" - the highest moral excellence</note> is a good quality of the mind by which one lives well, which no one uses for evil, and which God alone works within the mind. **Virtue**—

<column-break/>

—is a habit of the mind in good order. 

**Virtue** is a habit of the mind established in the mean <note>original: "i mēj" - referring to the Aristotelian "Golden Mean," the balance between two extremes</note> from our side. 

**Virtue** is a habit of the mind according to the form of nature, agreeable to reason. 

**Virtue** is a perfect quality of life in harmony with reason. 

**Virtue** is to do the best of the ordered, perfected thing. 

**Virtue** is that which makes its possessor and their work good. 

**Virtue** is a voluntary habit established in the mean from our side, defined by reason, according to that which the wise man would define. 

**Virtue** is a habit of the soul in the beauty of nature; the purity of the word of life, the service of the divinity of character; the honor of man, and the worthiness of eternal bliss. 

**Virtue** is a firm disposition or the highest nature of mortal life, praiseworthy in itself; or a completely praiseworthy good of the rational person; a precursor to good things, or a subjection to be confessed in all the conducts of wars; or a habit of prior-willingness for practical good or [beauty...].

...beauty. **Virtue** <note>original: "Arak'inut'iwn"</note> is the appearance and measurement of the middle path; or the noblest transformation. **Virtue** is incorruptible joy. **A Proposition** <note>original: "Arhajarkut'iwn" - can also mean "Proposal" or "Thesis"</note> is the praise of excellence. **A Proposition** is an explanation through reason, or a certain negative praise; or something [remaining] in the middle. **Health** <note>original: "Arhoghjut'iwn"</note> is the stability through which the firmness of nature is maintained.

<margin>[A]</margin> **God** <note>original: "Astvats"</note> is the sublime and self-existing Being; the supreme blessed Good, the Shaper of all and the Provider for those that have come into being from non-existence. It is an involuntary factual appearance born with it. **Hatred** <note>original: "Atelut'iwn"</note> is the will to distance oneself. **Hatred** is an alienation from held things that causes distress and drives [one] away.

<column-break/>

toward works that arise from external matter. **Art** is a composition resulting from the release of those previously trained toward something naturally useful. **Art** is a general knowledge along with its cause; or **Art** is a capacity traveling along with essence. **Art** is a composition from the capacity of the mind, previously trained toward some useful completion in this life. **Justice** <note>original: "Ardarut'iwn"</note> is the harmony of the soul; or the good order of the parts of the soul arranged toward one another. **Justice** is a capacity distributing with commonality; or a self-active energy of the soul's parts; or a lot of excellence, meeting the common good; or the knowledge of things to come and not to come. **Justice** is a nature with distributive power according to the merit of each person. **Justice** is a capacity of the soul to maintain the necessary benefit for the payment to each according to their merits. **Justice** is the rectitude of the will for its own sake. **Justice** is a grace-born covenant found for the benefit of the many. **Justice** is to serve those loved seeds and to rightly rule those [seeds] and one's own.

<meta>This page continues an alphabetical lexicon of philosophical, ethical, and grammatical terms. # Definitions of Virtues and Logic

**Justice** <note>original: "Ardarut'iwn"</note> is ordered love. **Justice** is politics: for each person to keep what belongs to them. **Justice** of the purifying [soul] is to keep the works of each individual virtue toward a single recognition: that is, toward justice. **Justice** according to the purified soul is to be so receptive to the higher mind that it forms an eternal pact with it, becoming like it.
<margin>relating to Justice</margin> **Justice** according to the Pre-ideal <note>original: "Nakhagaghap'ar" – the Platonic Form or Archetype</note> is eternal, and by everlasting laws it never tires of its own work.
The **Sign of Justice** <note>original: "Ardaranshann"</note> is the movement arising from the tradition of justice. **Results** <note>original: "Ardyunk'nn"</note> are the power toward good works in order to receive that which is determined. **Involuntariness** <note>original: "Akamaut'iwn"</note> is perfectly that which is not. **The Stain** <note>original: "Akasti" – a term often used for the blemish or 'crust' of sin</note> is the corruption of the image of God. **The Stain** is the loathsome filth that remains in the side <note>likely referring to the soul's "flank" or the wound of sin</note> from the works of sin. **Remorseful Cause** <note>original: "Apashawakan patcharh"</note> comes from the free will of those who have changed. **Unwillingness** <note>original: "Anakamut'i"</note> is when the same good has come to be. **Courage** <note>original: "Ariut'i"</note> is the nature of a soul unmoved by fear. **Courage** is the salvation of lawful glory; it is the [endurance of] harsh things and not the unwilling participation in audacity and ignorance; rather, it is the [willing] endurance of harsh things. **Courage** is to undertake the most difficult, high matters. **Courage** is a habit of the soul which

<column-break/>

[bears] all failures and loss of goods which are determined under our authority with steadfastness. **Courage** is the firmness of the mind against the temptations of this world. **Courage** according to the purified soul is to be ignorant of the passions <note>original: "kirs" – can mean passions, sufferings, or emotions</note>: not to be defeated by them, not to know how to be angry, and not to desire anything at all. **Courage** according to the Pre-ideal <note>the Archetype</note> is divine power, which is always the same and never ceases or changes. **Personification** <note>original: "Andznanavorut'iwn" – also "Embodiment" or "Prosopopeia"</note> is the drawing out toward the final, living manifestation of a place. **The Throneless** <note>original: "Ant'orh" – possibly meaning "un-seated" or "displaced"</note> is a living thing born in another. **Offspring** <note>original: "Ant'iwn"</note> is the progeny of the body. **Direct Speech** <note>original: "Anoren ban" – here used in a technical sense for "un-lawed" or "primary" speech</note> is orderly and functional, found in the common religion of the city or a specific group. **Air** <note>original: "Od" – likely a transcription error or technical term for a "Noun" (Anun) given the definition</note> is the gift of the body's mixture <margin>[Loose]</margin> and a signifying voice according to the will, with time, of which no part signifies anything when separated specifically; it signifies directly and through a single medium. **A Word** <note>original: "Barh"</note> is a sound that does not signify through composition <note>syntax</note> along with time; or a sound that signifies an action. **A Word** functions without syntax; but with syntax, they "sub-signify," such as "in Lycia." **Reasoned Speech** <note>original: "Bani" – from 'Ban', meaning Word, Reason, or Speech</note> is a signifying voice according to the will, whose parts signify separately; the power of reason, the thought of the cause and the caused for that same thing, and composed...

# Definitions of the Good and the Passions

The manifestness and pre-eminence of the laws signify the truth of voluntariness. **The Good** <note>original: "Bari"</note> is the pleasing will of God through right action, which is irreproachable. **Anger** <note>original: "Barkut'iwn"</note> is a boiling of the blood around the heart. **Long-suffering** <note>original: "Erkaynamtut'i"</note> is [the restraint from] causing grief to the adversary. **Anger** is a disorderly desire for vengeance. **Anger** is a movement of the mind accompanied by a disorderly desire for vengeance. **Anger** is an irrational impulse of the mind. **Anger** is the boiling of the blood around the heart caused by the rising of the bile. **Anger** is a longing to cause grief in return. **Anger** is a provoking movement of the soul toward punishment. **Anger** is the heat of the blood of the heart for the sake of causing grief in return. **Prosperity** <note>original: "Barebastut'iwn" — often translating the Greek "eudaemonia" (happiness/well-being)</note> is action according to the primary [nature]; the successful sufficiency of external things for a perfected life. **Good-Renown** <note>original: "Barep'arhaworut'iwn"</note> is the counsel of good things and not of evil; deceptive goods <note>This likely refers to the distinction between true glory and worldly fame</note>. **Good-Glory** is glory derived from good things. **Prosperity** is the disposition by which we determine what is to be done and what is not. **Resourcefulness** <note>original: "Barehnaraworut'iwn"</note> is the acknowledgment of the knowledgeable outcomes of actions. **Good-Orderliness** <note>original: "Barezardarut'iwn"</note> is the creative power of fitting and...

<column-break/>

...unfitting movements. **Piety** <note>original: "Barepashtut'iwn"</note> is justice specifically regarding holiness. **Good-Nature** <note>original: "Barebnut'iwn"</note> is a fitness of nature toward virtue. **Nobility of Lineage** <note>original: "Baretomhut'iwn"</note> is the succession of one’s own race in virtue. **The Good** is that which is called the communion of the laws of justice. **The Good** is that which all desire. **The Good** is that which is entirely pleasing to itself, or the useful, or the pleasing in this life, or the voluntariness of the rational soul. **The Good** is the most noble essence which the rational soul desires, following the likeness of the things longed for and desired. **The Good** is the joy of the soul, whether it be for the present or the distant future, attained with difficulty, even if it be a point of instability. **Moral Virtue** <note>original: "Baroyakan arhak'inut'i"</note> is the habit of the voluntary soul, or the training gathered toward virtue. **The Moral** is that which pays through difficulties, where the impulse of the mind occurs by judgment. The beginning of the **Moral** is choice, which is the appearance of the mind. **Moral Virtue** is that which has become tradition and commandment without [external] cause. **Order** <note>original: "Bard"</note> is the arrangement of the soul having entered into training. **Slander** <note>original: "Bambank'"</note> is the disfiguring of the good of others.

# Philosophical Definitions: Composition, Fortune, and Nature

**The forms of blame** <note>original: "Bambank'"</note> in every specific property.
**Composition of acknowledgment** <note>original: "Baghadadrut'iwn khostovanut'ean" — likely referring to a logical or legal confession/admission</note>.
**The composition of the moon** <note>This entry likely refers to the moon's phases or its physical makeup in medieval cosmology</note> possesses either remainders, or hallucinations, or a sensory habit that brings about its appearance. **Reasoned composition** <note>original: "Baghkhohaworut'iwn"</note> is the word by which those things acknowledged occur to the one who has acknowledged them, arising spontaneously through the very person who made the acknowledgment.
**The comparative** <note>original: "Baghadatakan"</note> refers to those things in which nature is expressed through [words] or joined together with fewer conjunctions. **By such compositions**, the "newly laughable" is distinguished from the "not-laughable," as in the case of mankind <note>This refers to the Aristotelian definition of Man as the "laughing animal," used to distinguish species</note>. **The desirable** <note>original: "Baghdali"</note> is that which is truly good or appears to be good, toward which an impulse is directed. **The appetitive faculty** <note>original: "Baghadzakanin" — the part of the soul responsible for desire</note> is the beginning of the impulse within the passions of nature; from the [soul's] threefold nature. **The release of desire** consists of spiritedness, lust, and the faculty of will. To follow after this leads to virtue and a decrease in their opposites. **Fortune** <note>original: "Bakht"</note> is a movement toward an invisible cause of a glorious thing. **Fortune** is a cause that is hidden from our will by chance; it is that which has happened or is impossible to reach by human thought. **Division** <note>original: "Bazhanumn"</note> is the primary cutting of a subject; for example, "Living Being" is divided into "rational" and "irrational." "Mortal" [is a further] division; "rational" is divided into "mortal" and

<column-break/>

"immortal." **Sub-division** <note>original: "Stora-bazhanumn"</note>: "mortal" is divided into individuals. **Rational art** <note>original: "Banakan aruest"</note> is that which is composed of words; however, **irrational [art]** is that which consists only of deeds. **The mouth** is the organ of the voice and of nourishment. **Medicine** <note>original: "Bzhshkakan"</note> is the art that brings health to diseased bodies; or the art of the purging and emptying of bodies. **Conduct** <note>original: "Beroyt'"</note>, or the medical art, [as applied] to the deed. **Medicine** is the art of caring for human bodies, being the creator of health. **Dissoluteness** <note>original: "Bghjakhonut'iwn" — often translated as lust or lasciviousness</note> is a great desire for shameful pleasure. **Dissoluteness** is the desire of a lecherous will. **Dissoluteness** is a foul passion, defiled by filthy longing and the breaking of the reins of service. **Dissoluteness** is an formless yearning of the body, boiling over against the rational [faculty]. **Dissoluteness** is a disordered love of pleasure which is shameful, characterized by gluttony according to the sense of taste. **Dissoluteness** is a lecherous mingling of the mind and body, arising from a foul longing. **Nature** <note>original: "Bnut'iwn"</note> is the birth of the realities of living things. **Nature** is an innate power; it produces the likeness of similar things in a visible way. **Nature** is the essence of things, composed as a dwelling, which reveals the likeness of the species. **Nature** is the lowest sub- [category]...

...nature <note>original: բերութի, likely the end of հանգամանաբերութի</note> [is a] creative-perfecting species [towards] the definition of the species. **Nature** [is] each substance that is able to act and to be acted upon. **Nature** [is] the principle of motion and rest in those things in which it exists, by itself and not by accident <note>This is a classic Aristotelian definition from the *Physics*.</note>. **Nature** [is] the distribution of measures. **Nature** signifies form; therefore, nature is a genus. **Nature** is that which belongs to active things; for some, it is a composition and a habit within itself, directed without imagination, and it possesses the principle of motion and rest. **Nature**, which according to the contemplation of the whole is the soul; but according to habit, it is like a certain art brought into being, for the perfection of plants and animals, and for the elements through their mutual transformations, in which the principle of motion is observed. **Nature** [is] what has begun from things acted upon and has not ceased. **Natural Virtue** is a gift which we call "well-spoken" <note>original: բարեբառն (barebaṛn), possibly implying "fortunate" or "blessed".</note>. **Natural Virtue** is that which comes from the temperaments of the body; these do not appear to one another but are called "slack" <note>original: ծոյականք (tsoyakank)</note> as they follow the order of the temperament. And [they are] irrational <note>original: սևանբանք (sevanbank), likely a corruption of անբանք (anbank)</note> because they do not know the cause of why they are virtuous; yet, even in irrational beings, there is virtue. **Natural Virtue** [is] that which has occurred without a cause from the temperament of the body.

The shape of the whole is simple and straight; <margin>it</margin> a single line supports it. **Spring** is the time in which moisture <margin>1</margin> prevails. **Lukewarmness** <note>The OCR reads "Գաղտնիութի" (Secrecy), but the definition describing a "diminishing of warm zeal" strongly suggests "Գաղջութիւն" (Lukewarmness).</note> is a deficiency or a diminishing of warm zeal toward the completion of a thing begun. <margin>7</margin> **Beauty** covers deceit. **Beauty** is the proportionality of the parts of the body along with a good color. **Superlatively Perfect** is that to which nothing can be added. **Compassion** is free assistance from the disposition of a benevolent mind for the sake of God. **Compassion** is that by which the service of blood-relations is performed for friend and foe alike <margin>ministry</margin>. **Discord** is opposition to the truth along with a reliance on the shouting of a faction. **Writing** <note>original: Գիր (Gir), can mean letter or writing.</note> is the smallest part of an articulated sound. <margin>7</margin> **A Line** is length without breadth, and its entirety is contained within two points. **Discovery** is the end of a search. **Wine** is water transformed in the wood [of the vine], or a warm and intoxicating moisture. **Wine** is water changed into another quality by means of the vine...

The **heavens** are a pellicular <note>original: կասկանմարմին (kaskanmarmīn) — referring to a shell-like or firmament structure</note> body, ever-moving. **Heaven** is the upper part of the world. **Earth** is a body, dry and heavy. **Geometry** <term>Geometry (Yerkrach’ap’ut’iun): Literally "earth-measuring."</term> is an art according to the magnitude of shapes. **Fear** <note>original: Երկիւղ (Yerkiugh)</note> is an irrational outward calling, or the expectation of a coming evil. **Fear** and **sorrow** mixed are anxiety. **Fear** and **encouragement** are confidence and boldness. **Terror** and **fear** of spirits. **Fear** is the expectation of threatening evil. **Fear** is the expectation of an evil from which one cannot flee. **Fear** is flight from evil. **Fear** is the love of a fugitive towards his opponent. **Fear** is a desire according to sensation.

<column-break/>

**Cowardice** <note>original: Երկչոտութիւն (Yerkch’otut’iun)</note> is to fall into decline from the weariness of opposites, with a broken heart. **Doubt** <note>original: Երկբայութիւն (Yerkbayut’iun)</note> is a word with two meanings; or [revealed] from many things, essentially and rationally. **Beatitude** <term>Beatitude (Yeranut’iun): Spiritual blessedness or the state of the "Blessed" in a divine sense.</term> is a grace not for everyone, but recognized by the truly wise, perfecting the conscience and [drawing it] near to glory. **Beatitude** is love of the supreme good, which one already obtains through delight. **Beatitude** is the enclosure of all goods, followed by the desire for the good of things that exist, and nothing else. **Beatitude** is the clear recognition of the Creator. **Beatitude** is supreme and ineffable joy, from which the wretched are estranged, and by which the Blessed one is entranced. **Quick-wittedness** <note>original: Երագամտութիւն (Yeragamtut’iun)</note> is the discovery of a confession, [happening] very quickly so as to be fitting. **A vein** <note>original: Երակ (Yerak)</note> is a vessel for blood. **Happiness** <term>Happiness (Yerjankut’iun): Often refers to temporal or human well-being, as opposed to eternal beatitude.</term> is the likeness of God according to human capacity, or a lifestyle of beatitude. **The face** <note>original: Երեսք (Yeresk’)</note> is the sensation that judges visible things. **An oath** <note>original: Երդումն (Yerdumn)</note> is taking God as a witness for something to be confirmed; or [swearing] by God according to the three times <note>Past, present, and future.</note>. **An oath** is a word that confirms itself.

<summary>This page contains a philosophical lexicon for terms beginning with the Armenian letter E (Ե), covering topics from logic (subject/predicate) and cosmology (heaven/earth) to ethics and emotions (fear, beatitude, and happiness).</summary>
<keywords>subjectivity, heaven, earth, geometry, fear, cowardice, doubt, beatitude, happiness, oath, Aristotelian logic, Armenian philosophy</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the alphabetical lexicon of philosophical and theological definitions in Classical Armenian. The previous page ended with a discussion on the nature of "Fear" (Erkiugh). This page concludes that section, moves through the letter Z (Զ), and begins definitions for letters E (Է), Ě (Ը), and T' (Թ).</meta>

...every truth. An oath is the testimony of an affirmation or a negation, **confirmed by holy things.** <note>original: Երդումն (Erdmumn). This fragment likely completes a definition of "Oath" from the previous page.</note>

<margin>1</margin> **Power** <term>Power (Zaurut’iun): Often refers to "virtue" or "potentiality" in a philosophical context, from the Greek *dynamis*.</term> is a proportionate co-mighty capacity. **Of Power:** a receptive capacity for the senses, or the readiness of the soul and body's will. **Power** is the name given to the soul and its organic parts in their capacity to support the productive receiver. **Terror** <note>original: Զարհուրումն (Zarhurumn)</note> is the fear of delusion. **Power** is the glorious height of the unshakeable mind, beautifully managing difficult matters. **Power** is the magnitude of a composite by which one lives in soul and body; a mighty wonder. **Anger** is a disturbed ill-will. **Sense organs** are the instruments of the senses. **Sensation** <note>original: Զգայութիւն (Zgayut’iun)</note> is the intelligible soul ordered from the ruling part toward the organs. **Sensation** is a passion of the soul by means of the body, which through the sense organs becomes an affection or a completion of a passion of the soul and body; or it is a type of phantom, or a sensory appearance. **Repentance** <note>original: Զղջումն (Zghch’umn)</note> is a sorrowful state taken regarding sins, having in mind that which was confessed and not repeating the act.

<column-break/>

<margin>1</margin> ...a living being that returns to itself. <note>This fragment likely completes a definition of "Spirit" or "Mind" from the bottom of the previous column.</note>
**Being** <note>original: Էն (Ēn) — corresponding to the Greek *on*.</note> is spoken of according to its equivocal nature.
**Essence** <term>Essence (Ēut’iun): Corresponding to the Greek *ousia*, referring to the fundamental nature of a thing.</term> is a self-existent thing that has no need of others for its existence; rather, it exists by itself. **The Essence** is the first substance of the body, the giver of cause to all bodies, the first subject <note>original: ենթակայ (ent’akay) — the underlying substrate</note> stripped of accidents; or that which is cast under every body.
<margin>8</margin> **The Universal** <term>Universal (Ěndhanurn): A term for general concepts that apply to many individuals, like "humanity" or "whiteness."</term> is that which is received as one in number according to the species of things.
**The Selective** <note>original: Ընտրողականն (Ěntroghakann) — likely referring to *proairesis* or free choice.</note> is the inclination by which the rational mind judges what should be done and what should not be done.
<margin>9</margin> **Kingship** is a monarchy for the common good, beholden to none.
<margin>10</margin> **Number** <note>original: Թիւն (T’iun)</note> is a genus according to the subject; the unit is its beginning.
<margin>11</margin> **Torpor** <note>original: Թմրութիւն (T’mrut’iun)</note> is a certain spiritual insensibility, a lack of benefit.
<margin>12</margin> **Arithmetic** <term>Arithmetic (T’uakanut’iun): Literally the "art of numbers."</term> is the art of the quality of numbers.
<margin>13</margin> **Estimation** <note>original: Թուեցումն (T’uets’umn) — often used to translate the Greek *doxa* (opinion/seeming).</note> Three things concern that which seems: for a count remains; all glory is an opinion in oneself. **Estimation** is that which is given to many praises, or especially to acquaintances, or according to their occurrences.

...distributing a portion like glory, an extension forward. **Opinion** <note>original: Թուեցումն (T’vets’umn)</note> is a weak belief.

**Time** <term>Time (Zhamanak): In medieval thought, time was often defined by its celestial cycles or its relationship to movement and growth.</term> is the fourth part of a year, or a twelve-fold division, or the growth of the person or the body through the years. **An Hour** is the measure of movement. [Abbreviated notation for the Hour and its recognition]. **An Hour** is the measure of the rotation of the first mover <note>Referring to the "Primum Mobile," the outermost sphere of the heavens in geocentric cosmology.</note>. **An Hour** is the number of the first and last parts of the mover.

**Temperance** <term>Temperance (Zhužkalut’iun): This term translates the Greek *enkrateia*, referring to self-control, endurance, or the mastery of one's desires.</term> is the power of voluntary, gathered labors. **Temperance** is the soul's precursor and the tearing away of prudence from bold encouragement toward death. **Temperance** is a virtue by which one directs every desire through the governance of counsel. ~~

<margin>Wisdom</margin> **Wisdom** <term>Wisdom (Imastut’iun): Defined here as both a human psychological state and a divine attribute.</term> is the vision of truth that brings peace to the whole man. **Wisdom** is the very highest knowledge of the divine. **Wisdom** is the recognition of the good along with the ability to provide counsel. **Wisdom** is the rational or discerning or distributing [faculty] of the image. **Wisdom** is the comprehension of the first and the cause-

<column-break/>

-al divine [things] or a divine-sent virtue.

**Philosophy** <term>Philosophy (Imastasirut’iun): Literally 'love of wisdom'. The definitions provided here are classical, drawing from Plato, Aristotle, and the Neoplatonists.</term> is the knowledge of beings as they are. **Philosophy** is the knowledge of divine and human things. **Philosophy** <note>The text uses "Wisdom" here as a synonym</note> is the meditation on death <note>A famous Platonic definition: *melete thanatou*.</note>. **Philosophy** is the likeness [to God] according to human capacity. **Philosophy** is the art of arts and the science of sciences. **Philosophy** is the love of wisdom.

**Understanding** <note>original: Իմացումն (Imats’umn)</note> is the intellectual operation of the mind. The **Intellectual** is that which is understood by the mind; that is, it is the intelligible.

<margin>E</margin> The **Liver** <note>original: Երգն (Ergn). This appears to be a scribal error or variant for 'Lyardn' or 'Erikaymn'. The definition 'indicator of food and producer of blood' confirms it refers to the liver in Galenic medicine.</note> is the tool of the blood and the indicator of foods.
<margin>L</margin> The **Tongue** is the organ of voice and of tastes.
<margin>Ts</margin> **Participation** <note>original: ծակցութիւն (Tsakts’ut’iun), likely a variant of 'participation' or 'fellowship'.</note> is a shared malice of two or many extensions toward something common... or according to a common definition...
<margin>Lnj</margin> ...such as a single living breathing being.

**Peace** <term>Peace (Khaghaghut’iun): Defined here as an internal alignment of the will with the "Good."</term> is the ordered tranquility of the mind agreeing upon the good. **Peace** is the state of the passions being ordered and tempered, in agreement...

<meta>This page continues the alphabetical lexicon of philosophical and theological terms in Classical Armenian. The previous page discussed various virtues and concepts under the letter 'Z' (Զ) and 'I' (Ի). This page focuses primarily on the letter 'Kh' (Խ), beginning with a multi-layered definition of "Peace" and continuing through "Conscience" and "Confession."</meta>

...of the eight parts. **Peace** is the union of those joined by love, or a oneness. **Peace** <term>Peace (Khaghaghut’iun): Following the tradition of St. Augustine, the author defines peace not merely as the absence of conflict, but as "ordered harmony" (tranquillitas ordinis) across all levels of existence.</term> of the rational soul is the ordered attainment of thoughts and deeds. **Peace** of the soul and the body is the ordered life of our salvation. **Peace** of the mortal man and others is the life ordered by faith under eternal laws. **Peace** among men is the ordered agreement under human laws. **Peace** of the house is the ordered agreement between the one who commands and those who obey. **Peace** that is heavenly, most supreme, and transcendent is the ordered and agreed-upon fellowship of those marvelling at the divine nature in God. **Peace** of the Orthodox is the ordered agreement of the faithful, and the consent of faith and the mysteries of the Church. **Peace** of the city is the ordered agreement of the citizens, between the one who commands and those who obey. **Peace** of the princes <note>The text uses "Amira," a term borrowed from Arabic to refer to rulers or commanders.</note> is the stewardship of their order. **Peace** is the orderliness [maintained] against instability. **Mixing** <note>original: Kharnumn</note> is a confused pouring out.

->**Humbling oneself becomingly.**<-

**Deception** is the opposite of truth. **Buffoonery** is a type of comedy—

<column-break/>

—a song that incites mockery or arises through hatred and words; it is the corruption of the mind and of the rational underlying judgment. That which is shaped within us between evil and good is the **Conscience.** **Conscience** <term>Conscience (Khightch mtats’): Literally "the scruple of the mind." In medieval Armenian philosophy, it is viewed as an innate, rational judge that recognizes moral law and evaluates one's own actions.</term> is a part of our understanding. **Conscience** is the rational judge. **Conscience** is self-recognition. **Conscience** is the habit of the soul regarding things to be done and things not to be done. **Conscience** is the habit of the mind through the underlying judgment of good and evil. **Conscience** is the soul's firm stance on doing or not doing something. **Conscience** is the law of our <margin>H</margin> intellectual [faculty]. **Kinship** <note>original: Khnamut’iun</note> is the closeness of persons through the flesh, stemming from a lack of complete fellowship in goodness. **Joy** <note>original: Khndrut’iun</note> is that which creates a pleasant temperament, yet a false opinion of certainty is difficult. The **Question** <note>original: Khndirn</note> is the necessity of logical words or the knowledge <margin>J</margin> of those who are ignorant; it is also a study that honors a small thing more than it deserves. A **Promise** is a word regarding a thing as if it were something new. A **Promise** is to do what must be done with humility. **Confession** <term>Confession (Khostovanut’iun): Defined here both through a medical metaphor—revealing a hidden sickness—and as a spiritual prerequisite for gaining knowledge and forgiveness.</term> is that through which a hidden sickness is revealed in the hope of forgiveness. From **Confession** comes the knowledge of useful things.

<meta>This page continues the alphabetical lexicon in Classical Armenian, moving into the letter 'Kh' (Խ). Having previously defined concepts like Peace and Deception, the author now turns to the definitions of "Sacrament" (or Mystery), "Prudence," and "Humility." The text relies heavily on Scholastic and Neoplatonic frameworks, citing Plato and Aristotle.</meta>

**Sacrament** <note>original: "Khorhurd" (Խորհուրդ). In Armenian, this term simultaneously means "thought," "counsel," "mystery," and "sacrament."</note> is a visible form of invisible grace. **Sacrament** is that which, under the veil of permissible things, works salvation through the secret operation of divine power. **Sacrament** is a material element presented externally to the eyes as a symbolic state; it exists according to a likeness [of the grace it represents], and according to holy beneficence, it is a giver of certain invisible graces.

**Sacrament** is a search for useful things, or the completion of deliberations, or the comparison of rational disciplines, or the disposition of good and evil.

**Prudence** <term>Prudence (Khohemut’iun): The text follows the classical definition of "recta ratio agibilium" (right reason applied to practice).</term> is the right reasoning of things to be done. **Prudence** is knowing what the soul must do. **Prudence** is the recognition of things to be rejected and things to be desired. **Prudence** is the knowledge of evils and goods, loving the one and shrinking from the other. **Prudence** is the rectitude of the reason used in action. **Prudence** is the choice between good and evil, and of those things which are neither evil nor good in themselves. **Prudence** is the recognition of divine and human realities, the rectitude of a good life.

The virtue of **Prudence** according to the "political" level <note>The author uses a Neoplatonic hierarchy of virtues: Political, Purifying, Purified, and Exemplary.</note> is that which, according to natural law, reflects upon the direction of all that one does,

<column-break/>

and wishes to do nothing other than what is right. **Prudence** according to the "purifying" level is to reject the world and all that is in the world through meditation on divine things, and to hold all the soul's recognition upon divine beings. **Prudence** according to the "purified" soul is to recognize only divine things, and to be in them as if nothing else existed. **Prudence** according to the "exemplary" <note>The "exemplary" or "archetypal" level refers to the virtue as it exists within the mind of God.</note> level is the Divine Mind itself, to which all things are naked and open <note>A reference to Hebrews 4:13.</note>.

**Prudence** is a creative power within the human being himself. **Sagacity** <note>original: "Khohakanut’iun"</note> is the good deliberation of human affairs, as **Plato** says; but according to **Aristotle**, it is a habit <note>original: "unakut’iun," a translation of the Greek "hexis," meaning a stable disposition or state of character.</note> joined with practical reason.

**Meditation** is the perfect inquiry to seek out things to be known.

**Lowly-mindedness** is the forgetting of one's own rectitude with caution.

**Humility** arises from the consideration of one's own weakness by [one's own] power; and it is a voluntary bowing of the mind before its Creator out of holiness. **Humility** is a [self-]blinding, or a bowing to the knees.

<summary>This page provides philosophical and theological definitions for the concepts of "Sacrament" (Mystery), "Prudence," and "Humility," drawing on the Aristotelian and Neoplatonic traditions common in medieval Armenian scholasticism.</summary>
<keywords>Sacrament, Prudence, Humility, Plato, Aristotle, Neoplatonism, Virtue, Ethics, Classical Armenian, Scholasticism</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the alphabetical lexicon in Classical Armenian. It concludes the section on the letter 'Kh' (Խ), specifically focusing on Humility and various forms of Moral Instruction (Exhortation), before moving to the letter 'Ts' (Ծ), covering topics such as Thirst, Laughter, Birth, and Sloth.</meta>

...always honoring the first of the wise and fearing him. 

**Humility** <note>original: "Khonarhutyun" (Խոնարհութիւն). In this context, it refers to a realistic self-assessment before God.</note> is that which presents itself in the passions in a manner appropriate to itself; it is the mind of the single-willed, being a leader in obeying those whom one ought to obey. And then, **Humility** is a virtue by which a person, through true self-recognition, appears lowly in their own eyes. **Humility** is the recognition of one's own weakness and insignificance.

**Instruction** <note>The Armenian word "Khrat" (Խրատ) can mean advice, exhortation, or discipline. Here, the author defines various modes of moral guidance.</note> is a word characterized by negation, urging one to abstain from certain desires. **Instruction** is the pondering of the word regarding something to be done by the will.
<margin>7</margin> **Instruction** is the subtle observation of the mind to examine and order things toward their final end.
**Instruction** is the remembrance of the divine will regarding higher things. **Instruction** is divine illumination and a gift to reveal His will. **Instruction** is a good and honest way of life, which does not allow anyone to do evil, but strives in those things which are done well to appear blameless. **Instruction** is that which, contrary to a [sinful] command, is the remembrance of the higher good to which we are bound. **Instruction** is the will to correct those who have erred.

<column-break/>

**Instruction** is the ordered rectitude of character and the preservation of the greater canons <note>Rules or laws of the Church</note> and of all things that proceed from them.
**Instruction** is the ordered movement of all members [of the body] and the proper arrangement of all habits and works.

---

<margin>1</margin> **Thirst** is the appetite for cold and moisture. 
**[Emergence]** <note>The text here is slightly fragmented but describes physical sensations: "Tsagk" (Ծագք) usually means origins/dawn, but the description follows physical distress.</note> is the nakedness of the body, the graceful stiffening of the breath, the stretching of the heart, the baring of the teeth, and the [narrowing] of the throat—the sounding of a wordless voice. 

**Laughter** is the breaking and fragmenting of the breath; and what is it else but folly and madness? 

**Bush** is a plant sprouting with branches from a root. 

<margin>40</margin> **Old Age** is the diminishing of the body.

<margin>50</margin> **Birth** is a certain transition from non-being into being.

<margin>8</margin> **Sloth** <term>Sloth (Tsulut’iun): Specifically refers to "Acedia," the spiritual apathy or "noonday devil" that causes listlessness in religious life.</term> is a sadness born from the confusion of the mind, or a weariness and bitterness of the soul, by which there is no spiritual joy in the Word. 
**Sloth** is a weariness of the inner good. 
**Sloth** is a concentrated sorrow of the mind concerning vain things and concerning...

<meta>This page continues an alphabetical lexicon of philosophical and theological terms in Classical Armenian. It transitions from the end of the letter 'Ts' (Ծ), specifically discussing sloth, to the letter 'K' (Կ), covering topics ranging from order and logic to biology, geometry, and theology.</meta>

...the desire of the body. **Sloth** <note>original: "Tsulut'yun" (Ծուլութիւն). In medieval theology, sloth was not just physical laziness but a spiritual apathy or "heaviness."</note> or the fullness of the soul; by which one becomes sluggish to begin the good, or grows weary of completing what has been started. **Sloth** is the distorted fall of the double man <note>The "double man" refers to the human person as a composite of both physical body and spiritual soul.</note> from our praiseworthy state of virtue.

<margin>Կ</margin> <note>The letter 'K' (Ke).</note>

**Order** <note>original: "Karg" (Կարգ)</note> is the harmony of things toward one another, giving to each number and ratio its proper place. **Order** is a certain sign manifest in those who are ordered, representing spiritual authority and discipline.

**Rigidity** <note>original: "Karkamut'yun" (Կարկամութիւն). This can mean physical stiffness or, as defined here, mental stubbornness.</note> arises from an improper and unbending hardness of mind, and a refusal to give way to what is better.

**The Essential** <note>original: "Karevor" (Կարևոր). This word now means "important" in Modern Armenian, but here carries the sense of "necessary" or "existential."</note> is the true essence in the midst of all things, which is beneficial to virtue. **The Essential** is being useful to the truth.

**Opinion** <note>original: "Kartsik'" (Կարծիք)</note> is general knowledge held without knowing the underlying cause. **Opinion** is a supposition based on appearances, according to such views of things.

**Milk** is nourishment for the living derived from blood, for the preservation of the body. <note>Medieval biology often held that breast milk was blood that had been further "cooked" or transformed by the body.</note>

**Perfection** is the attainment of the intended vision.

**Hypocrisy** is a secret inclination hidden beneath a shining exterior. Or, it is the display of a goodness which one actually lacks.

**Living** is the power of living things, which in itself is a creative quality.

<column-break/>

**Life** is the image of the Uncreated One.

**A Living Being** is an individual essence, possessing senses, established in the matter of a body.

**The Point** <note>A geometric definition.</note> is that which has no parts, for it is the cause of the circular shape; if it were to receive even one part, it would ruin the entirety of the spherical form. Thus, the whole [circle] encompasses it and does not allow it to spread to one side or the other.

**Woman** is the receiver and the first vessel of the seed.

**The Seal** <note>original: "Knik'" (Կնիք). In this context, it refers to the "Seal of the Spirit" given at Baptism or Chrismation.</note> is a sign of holy communion with the Divinity and the holy order of the faithful, given to those approaching God's blessedness. **The Seal** is the distinction of the eternal seal placed upon the rational soul according to the [Divine] image, making the created trinity <note>Refers to the tripartite soul (mind, word, and spirit) made in the image of the Holy Trinity.</note> conform to the Uncreated Trinity; it is the mark of the Re-creator, distinguishing the formless beauty according to the perfection of faith.

**Vigor** is a steady aptitude for confession and for all observation.

**Virginity** is the perpetual meditation on incorruptibility while within a corruptible body.

**Strife** is the movement of anger arising from a discordance of voices.

**Religious Observance** <note>original: "Kramnavorut'yun" (Կրամնաւորութիւն). This term relates to the disciplined life of a monk or a soul bound by a rule.</note> is the re-binding of our souls in order to draw near to the one God.

<summary>This page defines various terms starting with the letter 'K', ranging from geometric definitions of a 'point' to theological definitions of 'the seal' of baptism and the nature of hypocrisy and sloth.</summary>
<keywords>Sloth, Order, Opinion, Geometry, Baptism, Virginity, Hypocrisy, Classical Armenian, Lexicon</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the alphabetical lexicon from page 210. It concludes the final entry for the letter 'K' (Կ) regarding religious life and begins the letter 'H' (Հ), providing definitions for theological and logical terms such as Faith, Syllogism, and Patience.</meta>

**Piety** <note>original: "Krawnavorut'i" (Կրօնաւորութի). This refers to the state of living a religious or monastic life.</note> which [proceeds] from the higher self, offers a divine service and a certain unique ministry.

<margin>H</margin> <note>The letter 'H' (Հայբ). The manuscript now begins a long list of terms starting with this letter.</note>
**Faith** <note>original: "Havat" (Հաւատ)</note> is the substance of things hoped for, the sign of proof of things not seen. **Faith** is the permanent residence of the faithful, which situates the present in the truth and the truth in them. **Faith** is the illumination of the mind, arising from the Primal Light, to see spiritual things. **Faith** is to consider with assent those things which relate to the Christian religion. **Faith** is called a strong opinion. **Faith** is a rhetorical demonstration of conviction. **Faith** is a meditation regarding that incomplete hope, a journey of the mind with tireless eyes, persevering with endurance.

**Assent** <note>original: "Havanakanut'yun" (Հաւանականութիւն). In this context, it refers to the intellectual agreement to a proposition or truth.</note> is the liberation of the soul; also the agreement to the trials of the mind. **Assent** is that which leads to confession.

**Communion** <note>original: "Haghordut'yun" (Հաղորդութիւն)</note> is the mystery of divine and human love.

**Syllogism** <note>original: "Havak'abanut'yun" (Հաւաքաբանութիւն). Literally "collected speech," this is the technical term for a logical argument where a conclusion is drawn from two premises.</note> is a discourse in which, certain things being established and agreed upon, something other than what is stated follows of necessity from those things that have been established and agreed upon.

<column-break/>

**Understanding** <note>original: "Hanchar" (Հանճար). Often translated as "genius" or "intellect," it refers here to the capacity to perceive the deeper meaning of signs.</note> is the grace of comprehending the salvific meanings of rational or established signs. **Understanding** is the rectitude of the mind bound by causes within mysteries, or the firm establishment of the good within the person.

**Sagacity** <note>original: "Hanchareghut'yun" (Հանճարեղութիւն)</note> is that by which the soul observes this same thing.

**Comprehension** <note>original: "Hasoghot'yun" (Հասողութիւն)</note> is the composition of the power of understanding.

**Patience** <note>original: "Hamberut'yun" (Համբերութիւն)</note> is the voluntary and unwavering endurance of great and fearsome things; it is the cause of modesty and utility. **Patience** is [enduring] the evil of another with a calm mind; to endure and not to hold a grudge in any way against the one who does evil. **Patience** is the enduring victory over every suffering passion coming from without.

**Modesty** <note>original: "Hamestut'yun" (Համեստութիւն)</note> is the virtue by which decency gains for itself a pure regard for worthiness. **Modesty** is the restraint of the self, by which desires and pleasures are governed by law; or a moderate purity and good order regarding natural desires and pleasures.

**Proportion** <note>original: "Hamematut'yun" (Համեմատութիւն). This can refer to mathematical ratio, symmetry, or the proper relation between parts of a whole.</note> is the union of the necessities of the common good, for the sake of the good alone.

The **Equal** and the **Unequal**... <note>The text cuts off here at the end of the page.</note>

<meta>This page continues the alphabetical dictionary of theological, philosophical, and logical terms. ...of the genus, the smooth and the rough. Equality mixed with severity [is] the smooth, and inequality mixed with severity [is] the coarse. <note>These terms are used in Armenian Aristotelian logic to describe the "texture" of arguments or physical properties.</note>

**Wealth** <note>original: "Harstut'iwn" (Հարստութիւն)</note>—an abundance of possessions and belongings, or the attainment of sufficiency for life; or happiness.

**Necessity** <note>original: "Hark" (Հարկ)</note>—a cause of nature and from without, without which that which has happened cannot be tested. <note>In a philosophical sense, this refers to the constraints that make an event or existence inevitable.</note>

**Inquiry** <note>original: "Harts'umn" (Հարցումն)</note>—a request for an answer.

**Contradiction** <note>original: "Hakarut'iwn" (Հակառութիւն)</note>—the affirmation and negation [of a premise] which proceeds urgently; or [that which is] against justice without mediation; or words where one adds to or subtracts from the other.

**Oppositions** <note>original: "Hakakayk'" (Հակակայքն). This refers to the four types of opposition in Aristotelian logic.</note>—a fourfold difference: either as contraries; or as relatives; or as habit and privation, such as sight and blindness; or as affirmation and negation.

**Blasphemy** <note>original: "Hayhoyut'iwn" (Հայհոյութիւն)</note>—a word uttered in reproach of God. **Blasphemy** is to attribute to God that which is not His.

**Sloth** <note>original: "Heghgut'iwn" (Հեղգութիւն)</note>—a numbness of the mind by which one becomes indifferent to completing what is necessary.

**Heresy** <note>original: "Hertsuats" (Հերձուած)</note>—the private opinions of learned men [who are] in agreement with one another but who have voiced dissenting views. <note>Literally "a tear" or "schism," here defined as an intellectual divergence from the collective voice of the church.</note>

**Meekness** <note>original: "Hezut'iwn" (Հեզութիւն)</note>—the tranquility of a modest soul which is not moved by any disturbance of events. **Meekness** is the sweetness of the soul that is not overcome by bitterness.

---

**Meekness**—forbearance or sweetness which gives way to the wicked and knows how to obey the greater; it does not resist evils and agrees with good things.

**Meekness**—the benevolence of the soul which is neither provoked nor provokes; it neither harms nor thinks of harming. The **Meek** one is he who neither bears [ill-will] nor drags [others into conflict].

**Pleasure** <note>original: "Heshtut'iwn" (Հեշտութիւն)</note>—an occurrence in the sensory nature or a release from distress. But these are not perfect, for it encompasses only the sensory; for there are bodily pleasures which are of the senses, and vital ones which are of the soul alone. Thus, **Pleasure** and its activity are according to a natural habit, an unimpeded natural habit [as opposed to] sadness.

**Sickness** <note>original: "Hiwandut'iwn" (Հիւանդութիւն)</note>—the lack of proportion among the elements of the body: the hot, the cold, the moist, and the dry. <note>This definition is based on the classical humoral theory of medicine, where health is a balance of these four qualities.</note>

**Sickness**—the rot of the wood formed in the creative thrones. <note>A metaphorical or technical architectural term, perhaps referring to decay within the structures of a church or symbolic "thrones."</note>

<margin>M / N</margin> **Expertise** <note>original: "Hmtut'iwn" (Հմտութիւն)</note>—the [knowledge] of a single thing without its cause. <note>This distinguishes practical skill or "know-how" from "science," which requires understanding the underlying cause.</note>

<margin>H / N</margin> **Knowledge**—**Obedience** <note>original: "Hnazandut'iwn" (Հնազանդութիւն)</note>—the will to take up the burden of the greater. **Obedience**—a proper hearing, which is according to the canons of the Holy Scriptures and...

<meta>This page continues the alphabetical dictionary of theological and philosophical terms. ...according to the establishment of the Church, which we must [observe] with great diligence. **Holy** obedience: for it is the denial of one’s own will. <note>original: "kamat's" (կամաց). In monastic theology, true obedience is seen as the sacrifice of personal volition to a spiritual superior or divine law.</note> Obedience removes dispute. Sound: that which is specific and is received by the ears according to its own nature.

**Soul** <note>original: "Hogi" (Հոգի)</note>: an incorporeal, intellectual substance, receiving enlightenment; in its final relation, it is also in all [things]. **Soul**: the substance of the body, the governor of this body. **Soul**: the natural and instrumental activity of a body possessing the power of life. <note>This is a direct reflection of Aristotle's definition of the soul as the "entelechy" or first actuality of a natural body.</note> **Soul**: the life of the person. **Soul**: the god-like breath of life. **Soul**: an intellectual substance, [partaking in] double bliss in itself and in this body. **Soul**: it holds the center of the body, a natural instrument, receiving life by ordained power.

**Homonymy** <note>original: "Homanunut'i" (Հոմանունութի). **Fire**: a hot and dry body. <note>The author defines fire according to the four classical elements (fire, air, water, earth) and their qualities.</note>

**Sense of smell**: the perception of odors.

**Smell**: a vapor <note>original: "ogi" (ոգի), here used in the sense of 'spirit' or 'effluence'.</note> formed upon the "cooked" air, upon the more subtle and the thicker parts of the air; it is revealed to the nostrils through heavy breathing,

---
<column-break/>

for it draws the subtlety and thickness of the vapor so that it enters the person.

**Pride** <margin>1</margin>: a perverse desire for superiority. **Pride**: a disease, enraged and swollen with vanity. **Pride**: the arrogance of the haughty.

**Command** <margin>2</margin>: a determination for what we must obey.

**Angel**: an intellectual substance, ever-moving by will, self-governing, incorporeal, a servant of God, immortal. <note>This definition follows the standard Patristic angelology of John of Damascus.</note>

**Instruction**: the ordering of ancestral laws for each person; or as written truthfully by anyone.

**Voice**: a call coming from the mouth <margin>1</margin> of a living being. **Sound**: a striking of the air, improper and physical; or air struck, perceptible to the ears, and physical. <note>The author distinguishes between 'Voice' (articulate sound from a living being) and 'Sound' in general (physical vibration).</note>

**Crash** <note>original: "Chayt'ut'iwn" (Ճայթութիւն). Often used for thunder or the crackling of fire.</note>: the movement of air blocked and suddenly changed.

**Human voice**: an articulate sound which is revealed by letters and is written as a part of articulate sound.

**Animal voice**: an inarticulate crash produced by the windpipe. <note>original: "shncherakaki" (շնչերակակի), literally 'the artery of breath' or trachea.</note>

**Concordance**: [the harmony] between a ruler and those under authority; or the good disposition of the desiring part of the soul.

**Ordination**: [the laying on of hands] by one or by many.

**Truth**: A virtue by which events—past and future—are shown voluntarily and without change.
**Truth**: Rectitude which is understood by the mind alone.
**The True**: That which is is determined, and that which is not is distinguished.
**The True**: Multi-faceted; by being manifest, it serves as the true foundation of speech.

**Scientific [Knowledge]**: A virtue [accompanied] by cause, with elements following one another.
**Induction** <note>original: "Makadsut'iun" (Մակածութիւն). **Body**: A substance three-ways distinct <note>refers to the three dimensions: length, width, and depth</note> along with resistance.

**Body**: That which is resistant to the soul.

**Body**: A living, rational, mortal [being], capable of mind and genius.
**Is it possible to be without hindrance** as the philosophers say? Or can that which has not happened be true? Nothing impossible happens.
**Conflict**: The opposition of things in which it is impossible for both to be true or both to be false at the same time, such as day and night. <note>This refers to the law of the excluded middle and the nature of contradictory opposites in logic.</note>

<meta>This page continues the alphabetical lexicon of theological and philosophical definitions. It transitions from the letter "Y" (Յ) into "N" (Ն), "I" (Ի), and "Sh" (Շ). The definitions reflect a blend of Aristotelian logic and Christian ethics, typical of medieval Armenian scholasticism.</meta>

<margin>7</margin> **Providence** <note>original: "Նախախնամութիւն" (Nakhakhnamut'yun)</note> of God is a resulting submission, or the will of God by which all things receive what is appropriate for their existence.
**Foreknowledge** <note>original: "Նախատեսութիւն" (Nakhatesut'yun)</note> is a primary state by which things are seen before they come to be.
**Prediction** <note>original: "Նախագուշակումն" (Nakhagushakumun)</note>: A skillful movement showing what lies ahead according to necessity.
**Reproachfulness** <note>original: "Նախատատ" (Nakhatat)</note> itself: Bringing dishonor through words or deeds by means of cutting circumstances.
**To Reproach** <note>original: "Նախատինքն առնել" (Nakhatinkn arnel)</note>: To take revenge through contemptuous words.
**Envy** <note>original: "Նախանձն" (Nakhandzn)</note>: Pain at the good fortune of others.
**Envy**: Sadness because of the good of others.
**Envy**: Hatred toward another's prosperity.
**Envy**: Sadness if the good does not belong to oneself, though it is also a longing for blessedness.
**The Present**: A determination of the intellect that exists but passes away.
**Internal Reason** <note>original: "Ներտրամադրեալ բանն" (Nertramadryal bann). Likely referring to the Stoic/Scholastic 'logos endiathetos' or internal discourse.</note>: An understanding equal to the primary names within the person.
**Introspection**: Understanding according to one's own inner self.
**Action** <note>original: "Ներգործութիւն" (Nergortsut'yun); often used for 'Energy' or 'Operation'</note>: The movement of the maker which comes from within; this is authoritative. Conversely, anger is the **action** of the spirited faculty <note>The "spirited" part of the soul (thymos) in Platonic/Aristotelian psychology.</note>.
**The Contrary** <note>original: "Ներհակն" (Nerhakun)</note>: The opposition

<column-break/>

of separated things, such as day to night and evil to good.
**Innate Skill** <note>original: "Ներհմտութիւն" (Nerhmutut'yun)</note>: A remembrance and also a protective word of the mind of human nature, appearing in a constant and uniform way.
**Innate Skill**: A general knowledge without a specific cause.
**Material Cause** <note>original: "Իւթական պատճառն" (Iutakan patcharn). From 'iut' (matter/hyle).</note>: That from which the thing made originates; it determines the judgment of the thing made, or the essence from which the thing or the whole comes to be.
**Likeness to God**: For the holy and the just person to exist alongside divine foresight.
**Instruction**: A movement of delight according to likeness.
**Conjunction** <note>original: "Շաղկապն" (Shagkapun). A grammatical term.</note>: A significant sound.
**Movement** <note>original: "Շարժութիւն" (Sharzhut'yun)</note>: The action of the mover in the act of moving; by will, the will to move or things that are moved.
**Movement, the six types**: Generation, corruption, growth, decay, alteration, and change of place <note>These are the six types of change (kinesis) defined in Aristotle's *Physics*.</note>.
**Gain**: The growth of the person’s benefit by dissolving passions and making one prone to suffering <note>The text suggests 'gain' in a spiritual sense—overcoming the 'illnesses' of the soul.</note>.
**Tangible things**: The sensation of things felt by touch.
**Gratitude**: The recognition of friends and the benefits of others, having the will to repay them.
**Grace**: That in which the memory is contained.

<vocab>Providence, Foreknowledge, Envy, Action/Operation, Innate Skill, Material Cause, Conjunction, Movement, Gain, Gratitude</vocab>

<meta>This page continues the alphabetical lexicon of philosophical and ethical definitions. It moves through words beginning with the Armenian letters "Vo" (Ո), "U" (Ու), "R" (Ռ), and "Ch" (Չ). The definitions continue to synthesize Aristotelian categories (like "quality" and "habit") with Christian moral theology (like "mercy" and "gluttony").</meta>

**Quality** <note>original: "Որակն" (Orakn)</note> in its most perfect form was a state of potentiality; for quality signifies **capacities** <note>original: "զօրակութիւնս" (zorakut'yuns)</note> and what is adjacent to them—that is, heat and the heating power.
**Gluttony** <note>original: "Որկրամոլութիւն" (Orkramolut'yun)</note>: A disordered love of pleasure according to the sense of taste.
**Gluttony**: Filling the insatiable desire of the throat with every kind of food.
**Gluttony**: An inclination toward a visible good under the pretext of physical restoration.
**Gluttony**: Benefit that does not reach the capacities of nature.
**Gluttony**: A disordered desire in taking food or drink.
**Thunder** <note>original: "Որոտումն" (Orotumn)</note>: A sound joined to a cloud.
**Thunder**: The extinguishing of fire by a cloud.
**Swelling of the Mind** <note>original: "Ուռուցումն մտացն" (Urutsumn mtatsn). Referring to the "inflation" of the soul in anger or pride.</note>: A determination to take revenge for injustices committed against oneself.
**Rectitude** <note>original: "Ուղղութիւն" (Ughghut'yun); also "Justice" or "Righteousness"</note>: An equality of the will arising from causes.
**Rectitude**: A worthy repayment in the final results according to the impartial scales of reason.
**Rectitude**: A love of equality in those things where equality ought to exist.
**Habit** <note>original: "Ունակութիւն" (Unakut'yun). A translation of the Greek 'hexis', referring to a stable disposition of character.</note>: A persistent quality.

<column-break/>

**Vanity** <note>original: "Ունայն" (Unayn); also "Emptiness"</note>: Having hollow meaning or useless things. Or a futile effort, or the non-existence of all useful things.
**Vapor** <note>original: "Ուղխն" (Ughkhn)</note>: Formed from heat.
**The Brain** <note>original: "Ուղեղն" (Ugheghn)</note>: A part of the body containing within itself the bond of life.
**The Nostrils** <note>original: "Ռնգունքն" (Rngunkn)</note>: The organ of smelling.
**The Ear** <note>original: "Ունկն" (Unkn)</note>: The organ of hearing.
**Mercy** <note>original: "Ողորմութիւն" (Oghormut'yun)</note>: A virtue by which the soul is moved toward a misery that has occurred.
**Mercy**: A compassionate appetite of the soul accompanied by beneficence.
**Mercy**: An impartial and equal forgiveness toward everyone.
**Mercy**: A meditation of the soul suffering with those who are joyful. <note>The text says "khndaleatsn" (joyful), though contextually one expects "suffering." This may imply empathy in all states or a scribal variation.</note>
**Mercy**: Sadness at the evils of others.
**Mercy**: That by which we earthly beings gain heavenly things.
**Evil** <note>original: "Չարն" (Charn)</note>: A deficiency of the good, or a turning back from the immutable Good.
**Wickedness** <note>original: "Չարութիւնն" (Charut'yunn)</note>: To plot evils against someone.
**Evil**: That which is entirely avoidable in itself; or whatever is harmful in life.
**Wickedness**: A bad disposition <note>original: "ունակութիւն" (unakut'yun)</note> of the rational soul.
**Wickedness**: A disposition of the soul existing naturally, or the vow of a depraved soul.

<meta>This page continues the alphabetical lexicon of philosophical and ethical terms, moving from the end of the letter 'V' (Ու/Վ) into the letter 'P' (Պ). The definitions reflect a synthesis of Aristotelian logic (specifically the concept of "accidents") and Christian morality (definitions of fasting, marriage, and sin).</meta>

...[In vanity], a state not confessed <note>Or "unacknowledged."</note> in all conduct.
**Malice** <note>original: "Չարակնութիւնն" (Ch'araknut'yunn), literally "evil-eye."</note>: The evil will of a person when they are unable <margin>P</margin> to have anything else.
One is **reproached** for malicious testimony.
**Deficiency** <note>original: "Պակասութիւն" (Pakasut'yun). In a philosophical context, this often refers to 'privation'—the absence of a quality that should naturally be present.</note>: Distance from the honorable state in which one was naturally placed.
**Deficiency**: A state of lack by way of opposition, and not by means of incompetence or capacity.
**Terror**: Fear of an impending evil.
**War** is defined as the occasion for those who are <term>human</term> <note>The text uses "human" and "risible" (capable of laughter), which were standard philosophical markers used to define the essence of Mankind in medieval logic textbooks.</note> and capable of laughter.
**Accident** <note>original: "Պատահականն" (Patahakann). An Aristotelian term for a property that is not essential to a thing's nature.</note>: A distinct condition, such as the whiteness of a body, which can sometimes be a habit of it, and is not like the heat of fire <note>Heat is essential to fire, whereas whiteness is "accidental" to a body.</note>.
**Accidents**: Occurrences from both sides of a contradiction.
**Cause**: A command to act or not to act.
**Ambition** <note>original: "Պատուասիրութիւն" (Patuasirut'yun), literally "love of honor."</note>: A mad appetite for greatness or honor.
**Honor and Veneration**: Signs of virtue; the proper honor of modest voices, or the presiding authority of those who have been exalted.
**Occurrence** <note>original: "Պատահումն" (Patahum) - also "Accident."</note>: A being that possesses an essence which is naturally inclined to exist within another <note>Philosophically, an accident exists only "in" a substance (like 'red' exists in an 'apple').</note>.
He **reproaches** the slandering mouths

<column-break/>

of the wicked, as opposed to praise.
**Boasting**: A fervent state of mind, confessing from oneself that which one possesses. Or, [claiming] from that which one possesses as if one possessed it from oneself <note>i.e., taking credit for a gift as if it were an inherent strength.</note>.
**Service** <note>original: "Պաշտօնն" (Pashton), meaning ministry or religious service.</note>: Offering reverence and devotion to God.
**Worship**: The highest honor <note>original: "ծայրագոյն պատիւ" (tsayraguyn pativ). This is a translation of the Greek 'latreia', the worship due to God alone.</note> which befits God alone.
**Fasting**: The virtue of restraint and modesty regarding food.
**Preservation**: A virtue of the soul, by which we, through being worthy, advance toward a certain honor of worship for the Worthy One.
<margin>P</margin>**Impurity of the body**: To be mingled with it through deeds and lustful impulses.
But **[impurity] of the soul** is to threaten the faculty of choice by dwelling on these things. From which the holy <note>original: "սըրբինք" (srbink').</note> stay away, not eating with such people, but grieving for sinners as the Corinthians did.
**Fornication**: A lustful mingling between two free persons <note>meaning neither is married to another.</note>, whether it be with a harlot, a widow, or a virgin.
<margin>P</margin>**The Crown** <note>original: "Պսակն" (Psakn). In Armenian tradition, "The Crown" is the standard term for the Sacrament of Holy Matrimony.</note>: A righteous union of male and female, maintaining an inseparable way of life.
**Fruit**: The completion of a plant, by its nutritive nature.
<margin>P</margin>**Ambition**: A testing disposition. A successful undertaking.
