<meta>This page continues an alphabetical lexicon of philosophical and theological terms, focusing on the letter 'S' (Ս). It transitions from the physical and logical (definitions of genus and seed) to the ethical and theological (an extensive series of definitions for love, simony, and origins). The catchword "arra" (առա) at the bottom of the previous page correctly anticipates the word "virtue" (arrak'inut'yun) which bridges the two columns.</meta>

...becomes the instrument of bodily evil.
[It] is that which is a moist and cold body.
**Samekut'i** <note>original: "Սամեկութի". Likely a term for a base social order or unrefined state</note> in which the commoners and animals are masters.
**Definition** is a sudden, brief manifestation of the nature of a subject thing.
**Definition** is the limit of progress; it is that from which [a thing] is first cut and into which it is dissolved <note>In Aristotelian logic, a definition marks the boundaries of what a thing is and what it is not.</note>.
**Definition** is the circumscription of things.
**Genus** <note>original: "Սեռ" (Serr). An Aristotelian category.</note> is that which is predicated of many and different things in answer to the question "what is it?"
**Generically** colored: that which belongs only to the genus and not to the specific species.
**Seed** is a moist substance accompanied by breath, resulting from the transformation of blood, suitable for animal procreation; or it is an excess after the digestion of food; or the productive cause of every thing.
**Love** <note>original: "Սէր" (Ser). The following section provides multiple definitions of Love, from ethical to mystical.</note> is the growth of goodness toward those who insult.
The perfect [kind of love], among other things of delicacy, rejoices even in the sorrowful moments of death.
**Love** is the end <note>The goal or fulfillment.</note> of the commandments, from a pure heart, a good conscience, and sincere faith.
**Love** is the affection by which one loves God for His own sake and one's neighbor for God's sake.
**Love** is the life that unites the lover with the beloved.
**Love** is the virtue by which we desire to see God and be enraptured by Him.
**Love** is the vir-

<column-break/>

-tue by which the appetite of our souls is entirely upright.
**Love** is the most direct appetite of our minds.
**Love** is the harmony of minds, the fellowship of the chosen, and the blessed life of the souls of angels.
**Love** is having a good will toward all.
**Love** is having friendship toward God for His own sake and toward one's neighbor in God for God's sake.
**Love** is the virtue unique only to the righteous and the children of God.
**Love** is a habit of virtue in the soul by which we love God.
Out of outward appearance, placed within according to the appetitive desire for pleasure and inclinations, being a bond of affection for the beloved for the sake of loving, and not for oneself.
**Heart**: The vessel of receptivity, a shaper <note>or "modulator of character."</note>.
**Seed** <note>or "Life-essence."</note> is the beginning of animal life, or the essence of blood and spirit.
**Simony** <note>original: "Սիմոնականութիւն" (Simonakanut'yun). The sin of buying or selling ecclesiastical offices or spiritual boons, named after Simon Magus from the New Testament.</note> is the willing desire to buy or sell that which is spiritual or connected to the spiritual.
**Beginning** <note>original: "Սկիզբն" (Skizbn). Could also be translated as "Origin" or "Principle."</note> is every thing that is the primary cause of the existence of a thing, by which that which exists came to be.
It is the creative [origin], as God is to the world, and the [foundation] for a city.
It is the causal [origin], as God the Father is to the Son and the Spirit.
It is also material, as wood [is to a chair].

<meta>This page continues the alphabetical lexicon, specifically concluding the various categories of "Origin" or "Beginning" (Skitspn) before moving through terms related to vanity, mourning, holiness, and logical predication. The text transitions from the letter 'S' (Ս) to the letter 'V' (Վ) at the bottom of the second column.</meta>

...of the throne and the four elements of the world.
**[Origin] is** instrumental, such as the melodies, the hymns, and the lyre of the musician.
**It is** foundational <note>original: "նախագոյական" (nakhagoyakan), literally 'pre-existing' or 'primordial'</note>, such as the foundation of a house, the keel-timber of a ship, and the heart of a living being.
**It is** natural and legislative, such as the "beginning of wisdom is the fear of the Lord" <note>A reference to Psalm 111:10 and Proverbs 9:10</note> and the "beginning of a good life is righteousness."
**Vanity** <note>original: "Մնափոութիւնն" (Mnap'arut'yun), literally 'vain-glory'</note> is the willingness of men toward fame.
**Mourning** is the grace of grieving intensely for the growth of evil and the lack of good.
**Mourning** is the bitterness of grief, for the sake of taking vengeance upon evil from the bed [of suffering]; and from that which one cannot take vengeance by striving, instead offering compensation.
**Mourning** is a dominant transformation of the soul due to the loss of things desired, as when a desired life cannot exist or come to be; or mourning is a certain sensation of anger at the loss of that which gave pleasure.
**The Holy** <note>original: "Սբն" (Surbn), an abbreviation for 'Saint' or 'Holy'</note> is that which is itself pure, or firm, or free from the soil of the earth.
**Falsehood** distinguishes color, but does not itself provide color <note>Likely a metaphor for how a lie mimics the appearance of truth without possessing its substance</note>.
**The False-speaker** is a demonstrator of things that do not exist.
**Habit** is the fulfillment of irreversible training.
**Falsehood** is the false meaning of a sound, intended to deceive by design.
**Falsehood** is the veiling of the truth by word, or by deed, or by predicative

<column-break/>

movement. **Sub-predication** <note>original: "Ստորասութիւն" (Storasut'yun), a technical term in logic</note> is that which colors this and that.
**Sub-predication** is that which is applied to things.
**Predicative** <note>original: "ստորոգական" (storogakan), corresponding to the Aristotelian 'categorical'</note> is a demonstrative propositional statement; either following from acquired things, or from things joined together, or from things compared in an explanatory manner.
**Predication** <margin>or</margin> is a saying regarding someone, or predicated regarding certain things.
<margin>8</margin> **Irascibility** <note>original: "Սրտմտութիւնն" (Srtmtut'yunn), from 'heart' and 'mind'</note> is the aged rust of the soul through opposition.
**Irascibility** is the boiling of the blood around the heart.
**The irascible [faculty]** is a part of the soul; it is irrational and grasping.
**Holiness** is to know God and to do His will.
**Holiness** is the integrity of the two [parts] of man <note>Referring to the union of soul and body</note> preserved for the love of God by loathing [evil].
**Holiness** is the purity of the soul, loathing all filth.
**Holiness** is freedom from all defilement, a perfect and entirely spotless purity.
**The Miraculous** is that which appears highest and unusual; beyond the hope and the capacity of the understanding.
<margin>V</margin> **Usury** <note>The text here moves to the letter 'V' (Վ)</note> is the lending of money to extract interest.
**Usurious mixing** is [taking] something more than the daily loan...

--- Page 229 ---
<meta>This page continues the alphabetical dictionary/lexicon from the previous page, moving from terms beginning with the letter 'V' (Վ) to those beginning with 'T' (Տ). The definitions cover ethical, logical, and physical concepts, including usury, memory, the elements, and logical difference.</meta>

**Usury** is the tormented profit gained from a loan <note>original: "վաշխառութի" (vashkharut'i), referring to the practice of lending money at high interest</note>.
**The worse** is to be entangled in evil.
**Excellence** is the superiority of prudence <note>original: "վաշխումն" (vashkhumn); here implying a distribution or manifestation of virtue</note>.
**Recollection** <note>original: "վերստին յիշումն" (verstin yishumn)</note> is the development of a weakened memory that arises from forgetfulness. And another **recollection** is rational thought without formal study, as if it were innate.
**Anagogical hypothesis** is that by which the rational mind commands a certain thing to be <note>In medieval philosophy, "anagogical" refers to the spiritual or "upward" interpretation of a text or idea</note>.
<margin>V</margin> **Indulgence** is the courage of souls seeking pleasure <note>original: "վատակութի" (vatakut'i), sometimes used for effeminacy or luxury</note>.
**Confidence** is a disposition of words that does not come from previous thoughts or late ones <note>original: "վստակութի" (vstakut'i)</note>.
**Vengeance** is the primary power by which violence, as well as justice or harm, is rejected by being bad and by being a seeker of revenge.
<margin>T</margin> **The Element** is the first among things composed and things not composed <note>original: "տարր" (tarr), referring to the classical elements like earth, air, fire, water</note>. **The Element** is a principle from which something is established and into which it is dissolved. **The Element** is that which gathers and breaks apart a composition.
**Elemental** refers to each body composed of the four elements.
**Discord** is when the concern or will of many appears great through the voices of others.

ity among friends towards one another.
**Dissolution** is a lack of connection in the mind, by which a good that was started is completely abandoned <note>original: "տարալուծումն" (taralutsum)</note>.
**Indolence** is a lack of connection in the soul, which wishes to destroy itself by living in misery and poverty, for it is necessary to endure the labor of contemplating one's needs <note>original: "տարտամութիւն" (tartamut'yun)</note>.

**Difference** is a definitive completion.

<meta>This page continues the theological discourse from Book Five, moving from the general condition of the soul's pilgrimage to a specific examination of Divine justice, the nature of sin, and the philosophical distinction between the unchanging nature of God and the changeable nature of created beings.</meta>

<margin>in sins</margin> Divine justice, concerning the things of God, condemns only the one who sins as one who is not without guilt. In the same way, He condemned us because our heart sinned in all its sinfulness; and thus, it became evil. But through His own vision or His goodness, He brings the human being into a state of readiness. Thus, I will speak recognizably of his error; while he is humbled and numbed until life, I will speak of the sins of the proud, evil-doing people.

Remember them, so they do not go about haughtily, and I will speak of their error and the unjust wanderings of their sins. This is because when he hears the voice of the Lord, he does not at first recognize Him; and after the subsequent help of the graces of Christ, the middle part of the "house of thunder" <note>original: "տունութեանն որոտմանն" (tununt'yan orotman); likely a reference to the 'Sons of Thunder' (Boanerges) or a specific theological metaphor for divine revelation</note> was interpreted. The man, being righteous and willing, also maintains the orderliness of the world in these times. First, He tests from their novelties, catching the evil man with a rain of gifts for the sake of what has newly appeared.

To take upon oneself the sins through the graces of the Savior Jesus Christ, they began to disturb <note>original: "խաղամանել" (khaghamanel); an archaic term for stirring up or disturbing</note> the sins of the foolish, as much as they hastened to repent. Whence the rational beings are shaped in divine love; becoming manifest near each other, God is revealed, and they become sons with the small help of divine things—that is, they hope for comfort and protection. O, how much the prudent one, having declared with modesty, strives to avoid the error of the given things, to shrink from sins and to obtain good things. Receive the love of the responder <note>or "the oppressor," depending on the context of the struggle with sin</note>. Now within the persons: first, I spoke in erring forms, but then I stood fitly near this and was merciful in all things, according to the word, regarding the sinful part.

The first part also has sub-sections. He says that it happened until they were similar. The interpretation of his peace: the human being in justice. The third part further concerns only the things within his insults, their betrayals, and so forth.

**Now**, first, the natural state <term>natural state: refers to the 'natural law' or the inherent condition of a creature before grace</term> which is in the midst of evil must be changed. The lawless sinfulness remains until it exists solely in the heart of man. And in the three [depths] of hell, there is the beginning of good or evil. For change is characteristic of creatures; there is an unceasing change of forms in its significance. For they declare in such cycles all the knowledge of natural things.

But the Divine simplicity, being what it is, never changes in its nature; it remains unchangeable in its immovability. But in this, change belongs to us created things; it becomes holy, looking as if at His will; even the thing called "oneiric" <note>original: "օնիրեաց" (onireats); from the Greek 'oneiros' (dream), suggesting a visionary or dream-like state</note> arises. Immediately, He showed the goods of this promise. According to this, the unchangeability of created things does not change from that measure as he himself is punished for sin.

And further, as the Lord, also the small changes of the wills toward that heart fall providentially into worthy other words which were approached. For the likenesses of these become unchanging in the same changeable way; for his sake, the possessor or the one who remains, who had need, was changed into beneficent results. The likeness is the same. Concerning the help: would it indeed change in the same way? It would return to nothing if the Creator appeared in his various harms. And it is tested outwardly, as the spirit which remains in the matter.

Which is fashioned with remorse, that [state] and establishment, and this is defined more than the cursed thing. <term>Concerning</term> the sin and the Savior, whom the laws bring... the gates having descended to us theoretically, as a sign, as it were, it predestines the divine foreknowledge and [creation]. And the measure of the foreseer is not sought, until not reaching according to the laws. <term>Because</term> it is revealed: that by the extension of the revelation there is a measure like a habit, in which it is said to be a judgment of punishable honor. But if a characteristic of punishment is from nature, as if like the good, it has and takes; those divine graces become a compelling force. It is subordinated according to the nature of the compellers. The preparatory, original work, the perfecter of the mystery, wills from nature; so that in the good, the darkened glory of the original divine canon may appear to be seen. And from the beginning, by the <term>Divine Providence</term> <note>original: նախախնամութե (nakhakhnamut'ye)</note> of the Son <term>of God</term>, the nature [of things]... the sin comes in all. And also <term>for</term> which reason, not only is there nothing toward that error, but it establishes [and] helps... it is known by category. Nothing is far from having faith, both in faith and in the power of the proposition of the nearby care; nothing was set against predestination. Again, it is asked: what was the prayer which he sought before the sins? And what was the lullaby? <note>original: օրօր (oror); could refer to a state of infant-like innocence or a specific liturgical chant</note> To which also greater faith, which he would give through worship. <summary>This page discusses the interplay between human habit, the "clanging" nature of sin, and the corrective power of Divine Grace and Providence. It emphasizes that while sin stems from habit and the mind, divine laws (both natural and positive) and the "Narekian" path of repentance provide a means for the soul's restoration.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the theological discourse from the previous page, shifting from the mechanics of human sin and habit to the nature of Divine Justice and the "Ultimate Good." It explores how the Son’s foresight justifies the rational soul and concludes with a list of scriptural or liturgical references.</meta>

How does He justify some when, while being in the <unclear>hidden</unclear> fire, He calls the divine commandments "righteous judgment"? Similarly, regarding the Son's foresight: because the things that come to be by His will are righteous, He does not [merely] show righteous judgment, by which the things to happen for the rational being are foreknown. Therefore, even the punishment is truly for the sake of the good, resembling those who seek the same for the soul's stabilization. Having <unclear>read</unclear> the soul's radiance, and while You threaten, they do not observe what they set down. Then, after rising to the level ground, He honored them separately, then He granted grace; and not by means of harm [does He lead] to righteousness and severe punishment.
